QuARC / Point Track Data

Multi-view point streams
for robot manipulation

Abstract

57,459 robot manipulation episodes from InternData, LIBERO, RoboTwin and RoboCasa365, spanning nine dataset–embodiment pairs. Each H5-v6 episode stores synchronized multi-view RGB-D, camera calibration, persistent 3D point tracks, robot states and actions. The collection totals 237.18 hours and 10.18 TB.

Explore the data ↓View replays ↗

Video previews

Five distinct tasks per group. Each InternData group contains two samples from node42 and three from node43, using distinct plans. LIBERO includes one sample per suite.

Camera RGB and depth appear on the left; 3D point tracks appear on the right. RGB retains source brightness. Colored trails track persistent point IDs. Depth spans 0.1–3.0 m, clipped at the limits; invalid depth is black. Visible points use camera RGB, while other points are gray (static) or amber (dynamic). All 11,513 frames retain the original frame rate. Use fullscreen, playback speed or frame stepping to inspect them.

H5-v6 structure

H5-v6 structure

T is the frame count, A the action dimension, J the joint dimension, and N = 8,000 point slots. H / W are image dimensions; B is the total JPEG byte count for one camera. The structure below comes from the nine dataset–embodiment groups.

episode.v6.h5
├── @attrs                              Protocol, sample, task, robot, sampling and encoding metadata
├── timestamps_s           float64 [T]  Episode timestamps in seconds
├── actions                float32 [T, A]
├── action_valid           bool    [T, A]  LIBERO / RoboTwin / RoboCasa
├── cameras/
│   └── <camera_name>/
│       ├── timestamps_s   float64 [T]
│       ├── camera_valid   bool    [T]
│       ├── K              float32 [T, 3, 3]
│       ├── T_wc           float32 [T, 4, 4]
│       ├── rgb_jpeg_bytes uint8   [B]      Concatenated JPEG bytes
│       ├── rgb_jpeg_offsets int64 [T + 1]  Per-frame byte offsets
│       ├── depth_m        int16   [T, H, W] Affine-quantized depth
│       └── point_visible  bool    [T, N]
├── points/
│   ├── point_world        int16   [T, N, 3] Affine-quantized 3D positions
│   ├── point_valid        bool    [T, N]
│   ├── point_is_dynamic   bool    [N]
│   ├── class_id           uint8   [N]
│   ├── owner_id           int32   [N]
│   ├── geom_id            int32   [N]
│   ├── frame0_visible_view_count   uint16 [N]
│   └── reference_visible_view_count uint16 [N]
└── robot/
    ├── T_episode_from_robot_base float32 [4, 4]
    ├── joint_names        UTF-8   [J]
    └── joint_pos          float32 [T, J]

Fields & coordinate conventions

Field / attributeMeaning
contract / schema_versionsimulator-replay-v6 / 6. episode_length, fps and episode_complete describe sequence length, frame rate and completion status.
sample_id / dataset_id / scene_id / episode_idSample, dataset, task scene and source episode identifiers. language_instruction stores the task instruction; robot_model / simulator identify the embodiment and simulator.
coordinate_frameepisode_initial_robot_base: 3D points and camera poses are expressed in the initial robot-base frame, in meters.
K / T_wcK contains pixel intrinsics; T_wc is world_from_camera. OpenCV camera axes: x right, y down, z forward. Pixel centers are integers; distortion_model=none.
point_world[t, i]Index i tracks the same persistent point throughout the episode, bound to local coordinates on the rendered geometry. Decode with scale_m_per_unit and offset_m_xyz.
point_valid / point_visiblepoint_valid marks valid 3D positions per frame. point_visible marks per-camera visibility, checked with geometry instance IDs and depth. camera_valid marks valid camera frames.
class_id / point_is_dynamicclass_id: 0 = active robot geometry, 1 = dynamic objects, 2 = static scene. point_is_dynamic marks classes 0 and 1. owner_id / geom_id identify the owning object and geometry.
frame0_visible_view_count / reference_visible_view_countNumber of cameras observing each point in frame 0 and in the sampling reference frame. sampling_reference_frame identifies that reference frame.
actions / action_spec_jsonactions retains each source’s component order. action_spec_json defines names, units, control mode, frame, temporal alignment and final-frame semantics. InternData uses next-observation joint targets; the other three datasets include per-component action_valid masks.
joint_pos / joint_namesjoint_names defines the column order of joint_pos. T_episode_from_robot_base maps the robot-base frame to the episode frame.

Encoding & reading

Depth uses a 0.001 m quantization step; 3D points use 0.0001 m. Encoding attributes are stored on each HDF5 dataset. RGB uses JPEG quality 95. Import hdf5plugin before reading to register compression filters.

# JPEG RGB: slice the frame bytes and decode to RGB uint8 [H,W,3]
lo, hi = camera["rgb_jpeg_offsets"][t:t+2]
jpeg = camera["rgb_jpeg_bytes"][int(lo):int(hi)].tobytes()
rgb = np.asarray(Image.open(io.BytesIO(jpeg)).convert("RGB"))

# Metric depth: int16 storage, decoded to floating-point meters
d = camera["depth_m"]
stored = d[t]
depth_m = stored.astype(np.float32) * d.attrs["scale_m_per_unit"] + d.attrs["offset_m"]
depth_valid = (stored != d.attrs["invalid_stored_value"]) & np.isfinite(depth_m) & (depth_m > 0)

# Persistent 3D points: the same i is tracked along T
p = f["points/point_world"]
xyz = p[t].astype(np.float32) * p.attrs["scale_m_per_unit"] + p.attrs["offset_m_xyz"]
valid = f["points/point_valid"][t].astype(bool)
visible = valid & camera["point_visible"][t] & camera["camera_valid"][t]

# Project 3D points into the current camera
T_wc, K = camera["T_wc"][t], camera["K"][t]
xyz_camera = (xyz - T_wc[:3, 3]) @ T_wc[:3, :3]
uvh = xyz_camera @ K.T
uv = uvh[:, :2] / uvh[:, 2:3]

H5 structure JSON

Counting conventions

Episodes are manifest rows; tasks are distinct scene_id values. Duration sums num_frames / fps. Each render of a plan counts separately. Allocated storage counts unique inodes within each organized root. TB = 10¹² B; TiB = 2⁴⁰ B.
